)\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef git_repo(tmp_path: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Create a real, initialized git repository with one commit.\"\"\"\n    repo = tmp_path / \"primary\"\n    repo.mkdir()\n    _run_git([\"init\"], cwd=repo)\n    _run_git([\"config\", \"user.email\", \"test@example.com\"], cwd=repo)\n    _run_git([\"config\", \"user.name\", \"Test User\"], cwd=repo)\n    (repo / \"README.md\").write_text(\"# Test Repo\\n\")\n    _run_git([\"add\", \"README.md\"], cwd=repo)\n    _run_git([\"commit\", \"-m\", \"Initial commit\"], cwd=repo)\n    _run_git([\"branch\", \"-M\", \"main\"], cwd=repo)\n    return repo\n\n\ndef _add_worktree(repo: Path, path: Path, branch: str) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Create a real linked worktree of ``repo`` at ``path`` on a new branch.\"\"\"\n    _run_git([\"worktree\", \"add\", \"-b\", branch, str(path)], cwd=repo)\n    return path\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T002 — UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN (no claim, zero subprocess calls, NFR-001)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_no_claim_returns_unowned_no_opt_in(\n    git_repo: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"``claimed_checkout=None`` returns UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN with no git calls.\"\"\"\n    calls: list[list[str]] = []\n    real_run = subprocess.run\n\n    def _spy_run(args: object, *a: object, **kw: object) -> object:\n        calls.append(list(args))  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n        return real_run(args, *a, **kw)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, \"run\", _spy_run)\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(None, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN\n    assert claim.opted_in is False\n    assert claim.detail\n    assert str(git_repo.resolve()) in claim.detail\n    assert calls == [], f\"expected zero subprocess calls, got: {calls}\"\n\n\ndef test_no_claim_error_for_claim_maps_to_unowned_error(git_repo: Path) -> None:\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(None, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n    err = error_for_claim(claim)\n    assert isinstance(err, UnownedNoOptInError)\n    assert isinstance(err, CheckoutOwnershipError)\n    assert err.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T002 — trivial self-ownership (primary claiming itself) is OWNED\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_primary_self_ownership_is_owned(git_repo: Path) -> None:\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(git_repo, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.OWNED\n    assert claim.opted_in is True\n    assert claim.claimed_checkout == git_repo.resolve()\n    assert claim.resolved_primary == git_repo.resolve()\n    assert error_for_claim(claim) is None\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T002/T003 — valid linked (non-nested) worktree is OWNED\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_valid_linked_worktree_is_owned(git_repo: Path, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    worktree = _add_worktree(git_repo, tmp_path / \"linked-wt\", \"feature-a\")\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(worktree, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.OWNED\n    assert claim.opted_in is True\n    assert claim.claimed_checkout == worktree.resolve()\n    assert error_for_claim(claim) is None\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T003 — nested worktree (registry-based, not `.worktrees`-literal) is NESTED\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_nested_worktree_is_nested(git_repo: Path, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    outer = _add_worktree(git_repo, tmp_path / \"outer-wt\", \"feature-outer\")\n    inner = _add_worktree(git_repo, outer / \"nested-inner-wt\", \"feature-inner\")\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(inner, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.NESTED\n    assert claim.opted_in is True\n    assert claim.detail\n    assert str(inner.resolve()) in claim.detail\n    assert str(outer.resolve()) in claim.detail\n\n    err = error_for_claim(claim)\n    assert isinstance(err, NestedCheckoutError)\n    assert err.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.NESTED\n\n\ndef test_nested_worktree_uses_generic_registry_not_dot_worktrees_literal(\n    git_repo: Path, tmp_path: Path\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"C-006: nested detection must work for paths with NO ``.worktrees`` segment.\n\n    ``outer``/``inner`` below live under a plain ``tmp_path`` tree — neither\n    path contains a literal ``.worktrees`` component, so a detector keyed on\n    that literal would miss this case entirely (the exact C-006 gap this WP\n    closes).\n    \"\"\"\n    outer = _add_worktree(git_repo, tmp_path / \"generic-outer\", \"feature-generic-outer\")\n    inner = _add_worktree(git_repo, outer / \"generic-inner\", \"feature-generic-inner\")\n    assert \".worktrees\" not in inner.parts\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(inner, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.NESTED\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T004 — foreign repository (unrelated common-dir) is FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_foreign_repo_is_foreign_or_mismatched(git_repo: Path, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    foreign = tmp_path / \"foreign-repo\"\n    foreign.mkdir()\n    _run_git([\"init\"], cwd=foreign)\n    _run_git([\"config\", \"user.email\", \"test@example.com\"], cwd=foreign)\n    _run_git([\"config\", \"user.name\", \"Test User\"], cwd=foreign)\n    (foreign / \"README.md\").write_text(\"# Foreign Repo\\n\")\n    _run_git([\"add\", \"README.md\"], cwd=foreign)\n    _run_git([\"commit\", \"-m\", \"Initial commit\"], cwd=foreign)\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(foreign, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED\n    assert claim.opted_in is True\n    assert claim.detail\n    assert str(foreign.resolve()) in claim.detail\n\n    err = error_for_claim(claim)\n    assert isinstance(err, ForeignOrMismatchedCheckoutError)\n    assert err.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T004 — corrupted/broken gitdir pointer is BROKEN_POINTER (fail-closed)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_broken_gitdir_pointer_is_broken_pointer(git_repo: Path, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    worktree = _add_worktree(git_repo, tmp_path / \"to-corrupt-wt\", \"feature-corrupt\")\n\n    # Hand-corrupt the .git pointer file after creation (per WP guidance):\n    # point it at a gitdir that does not exist.\n    git_file = worktree / \".git\"\n    assert git_file.is_file()\n    git_file.write_text(\"gitdir: /nonexistent/path/.git/worktrees/to-corrupt-wt\\n\")\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(worktree, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.BROKEN_POINTER\n    assert claim.opted_in is True\n    assert claim.detail\n\n    err = error_for_claim(claim)\n    assert isinstance(err, BrokenPointerCheckoutError)\n    assert err.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.BROKEN_POINTER\n\n\ndef test_missing_git_binary_is_broken_pointer(\n    git_repo: Path, tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A missing/unexecutable git binary must fold into BROKEN_POINTER, never raise.\"\"\"\n    worktree = _add_worktree(git_repo, tmp_path / \"no-git-binary-wt\", \"feature-no-git\")\n\n    def _raise_file_not_found(*args: object, **kwargs: object) -> object:\n        raise FileNotFoundError(\"git: command not found\")\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, \"run\", _raise_file_not_found)\n\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(worktree, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.BROKEN_POINTER\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T001 — OwnershipClaim / OwnershipValidationResult / error vocabulary shape\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_ownership_validation_result_has_exactly_five_values() -> None:\n    assert {member.value for member in OwnershipValidationResult} == {\n        \"OWNED\",\n        \"UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN\",\n        \"NESTED\",\n        \"FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED\",\n        \"BROKEN_POINTER\",\n    }\n\n\ndef test_ownership_claim_is_frozen_dataclass(git_repo: Path) -> None:\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(git_repo, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n    assert isinstance(claim, OwnershipClaim)\n    with pytest.raises((AttributeError, TypeError)):\n        claim.opted_in = False  # type: ignore[misc]\n\n\ndef test_error_for_claim_returns_none_for_owned(git_repo: Path) -> None:\n    claim = resolve_ownership_claim(git_repo, resolved_primary=git_repo)\n    assert claim.validation_result is OwnershipValidationResult.OWNED\n    assert error_for_claim(claim) is None\n# Data Model: Worktree-Owned Root for Mission Create/Next\n\n**Mission**: `worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01` | **Issue**: [#3328](https://github.com/Priivacy-ai/spec-kitty/issues/3328)\n\nThis mission has no persisted business data model (it is a CLI/runtime-topology fix). This document instead models the **checkout-ownership domain concepts** the implementation must reason about precisely — entities, their attributes, and the relationships/validation rules between them, derived from the code inventory in `research.md`.\n\n## Entities\n\n### `Checkout`\n\nA single git working directory — either the primary checkout or a linked worktree.\n\n| Attribute | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `path` | absolute path | The checkout's working directory root. |\n| `git_common_dir` | absolute path | Result of `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` run from `path`, resolved to absolute. Shared by every checkout of the same repository. |\n| `toplevel` | absolute path | Result of `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` run from `path`. |\n| `is_primary` | bool | True iff `path == git_common_dir.parent` (the checkout that owns `.git` as a real directory, not a `gitdir:` pointer file). |\n| `gitdir_pointer_target` | absolute path \\| None | For a linked worktree, the target of the `.git` file's `gitdir:` line (e.g. `<primary>/.git/worktrees/<name>`). `None` for the primary checkout. |\n\n**Existing code that computes these attributes** (research D-2, D-4): `is_worktree_context()`, `_is_worktree_of()`, `locate_project_root()`, `resolve_canonical_repo_root()` — each recomputes a subset independently; the plan should route new validation through `_is_worktree_of`'s comparison rather than adding a sixth implementation.\n\n### `OwnershipClaim`\n\nThe explicit, per-invocation declaration that a `Checkout` should be treated as the mission's write root.\n\n| Attribute | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `claimed_checkout` | `Checkout` | The checkout the caller has explicitly named (new — does not exist today). |\n| `resolved_primary` | `Checkout` | The canonical common-repository checkout, resolved independently of the claim (via the existing `locate_project_root`/`get_main_repo_root` family). |\n| `validation_result` | `OwnershipValidationResult` (enum, below) | Outcome of comparing `claimed_checkout` against `resolved_primary`'s topology. |\n| `opted_in` | bool | Whether the caller supplied the new explicit-ownership affordance at all. When `False`, today's existing fail-closed default behavior (research D-1) applies unchanged — no `OwnershipClaim` is constructed. |\n\n### `OwnershipValidationResult` (enum)\n\nThe five possible validation outcomes (spec.md Key Entities, User Story 2's acceptance scenarios):\n\n| Value | Meaning | Existing precedent |\n|---|---|---|\n| `OWNED` | `claimed_checkout`'s `git_common_dir` matches `resolved_primary`'s `git_common_dir`, and `claimed_checkout` is not nested inside any other worktree per the registry. Accepted. | `_is_worktree_of()` returning `True`, plus a new nested-worktree negative check (research D-4). |\n| `UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN` | Caller did not supply the ownership affordance; today's existing refusal (mission create) or ambient behavior (next) applies unchanged. | Existing `is_worktree_context()` / `require_main_repo` behavior — UNCHANGED (C-001, FR-004). |\n| `NESTED` | `claimed_checkout`'s path is a descendant of another worktree's root per `git worktree list --porcelain`. Refused. | New — built on `coordination/surface_resolver.read_worktree_registry()`'s raw entries (research D-4), not its `.worktrees`-literal `_enclosing_worktree_root()`. |\n| `FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED` | `claimed_checkout`'s `git_common_dir` does not match `resolved_primary`'s. Refused, common-dir named in the error. | `_is_worktree_of()` returning `False` on a common-dir mismatch. |\n| `BROKEN_POINTER` | `claimed_checkout`'s `.git` file exists but its `gitdir:` target is missing/unreadable, or any underlying git subprocess failed. Refused (fail-closed, NFR-004). | New — must not raise an unstructured `KeyError`/`FileNotFoundError`; wrap as a named error. |\n\n### `PerCheckoutRuntimeState`\n\nRuntime bookkeeping that must be rooted under the `OwnershipClaim.claimed_checkout` rather than `resolved_primary` once ownership is `OWNED` (research D-6).\n\n| Attribute | Type | Description |\n|---|---|---|\n| `feature_runs_path` | path | `<claimed_checkout>/.kittify/runtime/feature-runs.json` (today always `<resolved_primary>/...` — FR-007 fix target). |\n| `merge_runtime_dir` | path | `<claimed_checkout>/.kittify/runtime/merge/<mission_id>/` (today always `<resolved_primary>/...`). |\n\n**Explicitly excluded** from this entity (research D-6): the cross-worktree status lock (`status/locking.py`'s `spec-kitty-locks/<mission_slug>.status.lock`) stays rooted at the shared `git_common_dir` by design — it is a different concern (serializing convergent writes to shared planning artifacts, not per-checkout isolation) and is out of scope for relocation.\n\n## Relationships\n\n```\nCheckout (1) ──is_common_dir_peer_of──> Checkout (0..N)   [same git_common_dir; e.g. primary + N linked worktrees]\nOwnershipClaim ──references── claimed_checkout: Checkout\nOwnershipClaim ──references── resolved_primary: Checkout\nOwnershipClaim ──produces── validation_result: OwnershipValidationResult\nOwnershipClaim (validation_result=OWNED) ──scopes── PerCheckoutRuntimeState\nOwnershipClaim (validation_result=OWNED) ──scopes── safe_commit(repo_root=resolved_primary, worktree_root=claimed_checkout)\n```\n\n## Validation Rules (drive FR-003/FR-005/FR-006/NFR-004 directly)\n\n1. An `OwnershipClaim` is only constructed when the caller opts in explicitly (`opted_in=True`); absent that, behavior is byte-identical to today (C-001/C-002/FR-004).\n2. `resolved_primary` MUST be computed independently of `claimed_checkout` (via the existing ambient-collapse resolvers) — never derived FROM the claim — so a claim cannot self-certify.\n3. Common-dir comparison (`OWNED` vs `FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED`) MUST use the fail-closed comparator (`_is_worktree_of`'s internal logic: any `None` from a git subprocess call → refuse), never the non-fail-closed `status/locking._git_common_dir` fallback pattern (NFR-004).\n4. Nested-worktree detection (`NESTED`) MUST be evaluated using the raw `git worktree list --porcelain` registry entries' paths (ancestor/descendant path comparison), not the `.worktrees`-literal `_enclosing_worktree_root()` helper (C-006).\n5. Every non-`OWNED` result MUST raise/return a structurally distinguishable error (FR-011) — not a single generic string match on `\"worktree\"` (the current UX hint in `mission_create.py:_print_worktree_navigation_hint` string-matches this way and must not be the ONLY signal for the new refusal classes).\n6. `PerCheckoutRuntimeState` paths are only used when `validation_result == OWNED`; otherwise runtime state continues to resolve against `resolved_primary` exactly as today.\n# Contract: Explicit Checkout-Ownership Affordance\n\n**Mission**: `worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01` | **Issue**: [#3328](https://github.com/Priivacy-ai/spec-kitty/issues/3328)\n\nThis contract defines the CLI/API surface the implementation phase (WPs) must deliver. It is a specification of observable behavior — exact flag names/wording are implementation-phase decisions the tasks phase may refine, but the request/response shape and refusal taxonomy below are binding.\n\n## CLI Surface: `spec-kitty agent mission create`\n\n### New option\n\n```\n--owned-checkout PATH   Explicitly declare PATH as a linked worktree this invocation\n                         owns. Validated via git topology (common-dir + worktree\n                         registry) before acceptance. Mutually exclusive with running\n                         from an unowned worktree without this flag (existing refusal\n                         is unchanged when this flag is absent).\n```\n\n### Behavior contract\n\n| Precondition | `--owned-checkout` supplied? | Outcome |\n|---|---|---|\n| CWD is the primary checkout | No | Unchanged — succeeds as today. |\n| CWD is the primary checkout | Yes, path == CWD | Succeeds (trivial self-ownership case, mirrors `safe_commit`'s `repo_root == worktree_root` contract). |\n| CWD is a linked worktree (any path) | No | Unchanged — refuses with today's `MissionCreationError` (\"Cannot create missions from inside a worktree…\"). |\n| CWD is a linked worktree, path is a validly-linked, non-nested worktree of the resolved primary | Yes, path == CWD | Succeeds. Mission files, coordination/lane refs land under `path`. Primary checkout and every other worktree remain untouched. |\n| Path is nested inside another worktree's checkout | Yes | Refuses. Error is structurally distinguishable as `NESTED` (see Error Taxonomy). |\n| Path's git common-dir does not match the resolved primary's | Yes | Refuses. Error is structurally distinguishable as `FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED`, naming both common-dirs. |\n| Path's `.git` file exists but its `gitdir:` target is unreadable/missing | Yes | Refuses. Error is structurally distinguishable as `BROKEN_POINTER`. |\n| Any underlying `git rev-parse`/`git worktree list` subprocess fails | Yes | Refuses (fail-closed, NFR-004) — never falls back to unvalidated acceptance. |\n\n### `--json` payload shape (success)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"success\": true,\n  \"mission_slug\": \"...\",\n  \"owned_checkout\": \"/abs/path/to/worktree\",\n  \"canonical_repo_root\": \"/abs/path/to/primary\",\n  ...\n}\n```\n\n### `--json` payload shape (refusal)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"success\": false,\n  \"error_code\": \"OWNERSHIP_NESTED\" | \"OWNERSHIP_FOREIGN\" | \"OWNERSHIP_BROKEN_POINTER\" | \"WORKTREE_INVOCATION_REFUSED\",\n  \"error\": \"<human-readable message naming the specific checkouts/paths involved>\"\n}\n```\n\n`WORKTREE_INVOCATION_REFUSED` is the existing (unchanged) refusal code for the no-opt-in case — this contract does not rename or restructure it (regression risk called out in plan.md IC-04).\n\n## CLI Surface: `spec-kitty next`\n\n### New option\n\n```\n--owned-checkout PATH   Same semantics as `mission create`'s flag. When supplied and\n                         validated OWNED, `next` roots per-checkout runtime state\n                         (feature-runs.json, merge-lock directory) at PATH instead of\n                         the ambiently-resolved primary checkout.\n```\n\n### Behavior contract\n\n| Precondition | `--owned-checkout` supplied? | Outcome |\n|---|---|---|\n| CWD is a `.worktrees/<name>` path (today's `require_main_repo` detects this) | No | Unchanged — refuses exactly as today. |\n| CWD is a generic linked worktree (today's `require_main_repo` does NOT detect this) | No | Unchanged — behaves exactly as today (ambient resolution to primary; NOT retrofitted into a new refusal — spec.md Acceptance Scenario, User Story 2 #4). |\n| CWD is a validly-linked, non-nested worktree | Yes, path == CWD | Succeeds. Mission decision resolves against the mission stored at the owned checkout; runtime-state files (`feature-runs.json` equivalent) are read/written under the owned checkout's own `.kittify/runtime/`. |\n| Nested / foreign / broken-pointer (same taxonomy as `mission create`) | Yes | Refuses with the same structured error codes. |\n\n## Error Taxonomy (shared by both surfaces)\n\n| `error_code` | `OwnershipValidationResult` (data-model.md) | Meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `WORKTREE_INVOCATION_REFUSED` | `UNOWNED_NO_OPT_IN` | Existing, unchanged refusal — caller did not opt in. |\n| `OWNERSHIP_NESTED` | `NESTED` | The claimed checkout is nested inside another worktree's own directory tree. |\n| `OWNERSHIP_FOREIGN` | `FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED` | The claimed checkout's git common-dir does not match the resolved primary's. |\n| `OWNERSHIP_BROKEN_POINTER` | `BROKEN_POINTER` | The claimed checkout's `.git` gitdir pointer is unreadable, missing its target, or a git subprocess failed while validating it. |\n\n## Non-Goals of This Contract\n\n- Does not define `#3128`'s post-ownership caller-vs-declared-workspace comparison (that guard consumes this contract's validated `OwnershipClaim` as an input, in a separate mission).\n- Does not add any new production `allow_worktree_context=True` call site (NFR-003) — `--owned-checkout` is a wholly independent, validated parameter.\n- Does not change behavior for any caller that omits `--owned-checkout` (C-001/C-002).\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        raise SafeCommitBackstopError(\n            unexpected=(UnexpectedStagedPath(path=\"<probe-failed>\", status_code=\"??\"),),\n            requested=tuple(expected_paths),\n            worktree_root=repo_path,\n            destination_ref=_current_branch(repo_path),\n            head_sha=_run_git_text(repo_path, [\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"]),\n        )\n\n    expected_set = {to_posix(p) for p in expected_paths}\n    unexpected: list[UnexpectedStagedPath] = []\n    for raw_line in result.stdout.splitlines():\n        line = raw_line.strip()\n        if not line:\n            continue\n        parts = line.split(\"\\t\", 1)\n        if len(parts) != 2:\n            continue\n        status_code, staged_path = parts\n        normalized = to_posix(staged_path)\n        if normalized not in expected_set:\n            unexpected.append(\n                UnexpectedStagedPath(path=normalized, status_code=f\"{status_code} \"),\n            )\n\n    if unexpected:\n        raise SafeCommitBackstopError(\n            unexpected=tuple(unexpected),\n            requested=tuple(expected_set),\n            worktree_root=repo_path,\n            destination_ref=_current_branch(repo_path),\n            head_sha=_run_git_text(repo_path, [\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"]),\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Destination-ref-aware safe_commit\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _read_worktree_head(worktree_root: Path) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the short branch name at worktree HEAD, or ``None`` if detached.\"\"\"\n    raw = _run_git_text(worktree_root, [\"symbolic-ref\", \"HEAD\"])\n    if raw is None:\n        return None\n    return raw.removeprefix(\"refs/heads/\")\n\n\ndef _is_worktree_of(repo_root: Path, worktree_root: Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return ``True`` iff ``worktree_root`` is a worktree of ``repo_root``.\n\n    Uses ``git -C <worktree_root> rev-parse --show-toplevel`` to confirm\n    ``worktree_root`` is inside *some* git working tree, then compares the\n    common dir of ``worktree_root`` and ``repo_root`` — if they share a common\n    ``.git`` repository, they are linked. A failing rev-parse means\n    ``worktree_root`` is not a git worktree at all.\n    \"\"\"\n    toplevel = _run_git_text(worktree_root, [\"rev-parse\", \"--show-toplevel\"])\n    if toplevel is None:\n        return False\n    # If worktree_root and repo_root resolve to the same directory, they are\n    # trivially \"the same\" worktree.\n    if Path(toplevel).resolve() == repo_root.resolve() == worktree_root.resolve():\n        return True\n    # Otherwise, they must share the same common git dir.\n    wt_common = _run_git_text(worktree_root, [\"rev-parse\", \"--git-common-dir\"])\n    repo_common = _run_git_text(repo_root, [\"rev-parse\", \"--git-common-dir\"])\n    if wt_common is None or repo_common is None:\n        return False\n    # rev-parse --git-common-dir may return relative paths; resolve them\n    # against the respective working dirs.\n    wt_common_path = (worktree_root / wt_common).resolve()\n    repo_common_path = (repo_root / repo_common).resolve()\n    return wt_common_path == repo_common_path\n\n\ndef _destination_ref_exists(worktree_root: Path, destination_ref: str) -> bool:\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"rev-parse\", \"--verify\", \"--quiet\", f\"refs/heads/{destination_ref}\"],\n        cwd=worktree_root,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    return result.returncode == 0\n\n\ndef _find_stash_ref(repo_path: Path, stash_message: str) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the stash ref for a unique stash message, if present.\"\"\"\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"stash\", \"list\", \"--format=%gd\\t%s\"],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        return None\n\n    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():\n        if \"\\t\" not in line:\n            continue\n        ref, message = line.split(\"\\t\", 1)\n        if message == stash_message or message.endswith(f\": {stash_message}\"):\n            return ref\n\n    return None\n\n\ndef _stage_requested_files(repo_path: Path, normalized_files: list[str]) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Stage each requested file via ``git add --force``. Returns False on failure.\"\"\"\n    for file_path in normalized_files:\n        add_result = subprocess.run(\n            [\"git\", \"add\", \"--force\", \"--\", file_path],\n            cwd=repo_path,\n            capture_output=True,\n            f\"Flatten the mission — drop the `coordination_branch` key from \"\n            f\"meta.json — to recover: run `spec-kitty doctor coordination --fix` \"\n            f\"to flatten automatically, or remove the key manually and run \"\n            f\"`spec-kitty migrate backfill-topology`.\"\n        )\n        super().__init__(\n            repo_root=repo_root,\n            mission_slug=mission_slug,\n            mid8=mid8,\n            coord_candidate=coord_candidate,\n            primary_candidate=primary_candidate,\n        )\n\n    def __str__(self) -> str:  # pragma: no cover - trivial formatting\n        return (\n            f\"Coordination branch {self.coordination_branch!r} for mission \"\n            f\"{self.mission_slug!r} is declared in meta.json but deleted from \"\n            f\"git. {self.next_step}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef read_worktree_registry(repo_root: Path) -> frozenset[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Return resolved paths registered in ``git worktree list --porcelain``.\n\n    The single authority for \"is this path a registered worktree\". Fails closed\n    via :class:`WorktreeRegistryUnavailable` when git cannot be consulted —\n    name-derived guessing is never substituted (NFR-003). Exemplar:\n    ``cli/commands/doctor.py:~3063`` (the cache-once-per-pass pattern).\n\n    Batch callers (dashboard scanner, status_service contract routing) read this\n    once and pass the result as ``registry=`` to :func:`classify_worktree_topology`\n    rather than re-shelling per path.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [\"git\", \"-C\", str(repo_root), \"worktree\", \"list\", \"--porcelain\"],\n            check=False,\n            capture_output=True,\n            text=True,\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n            errors=\"replace\",\n        )\n    except OSError as exc:  # git missing / not executable\n        raise WorktreeRegistryUnavailable(repo_root=repo_root, detail=str(exc)) from exc\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        detail = (\n            result.stderr.strip()\n            or result.stdout.strip()\n            or f\"exit {result.returncode}\"\n        )\n        raise WorktreeRegistryUnavailable(repo_root=repo_root, detail=detail)\n    registered: set[Path] = set()\n    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():\n        if line.startswith(\"worktree \"):\n            raw = line[len(\"worktree \") :].strip()\n            try:\n                registered.add(Path(raw).resolve())\n            except OSError:\n                continue\n    return frozenset(registered)\n\n\ndef is_under_worktrees_segment(path: Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return whether *path* lives under a ``.worktrees`` segment (shape only).\n\n    The blessed home for the ``\".worktrees\" in parts`` idiom (C-SEAM-1). This is\n    a pure *shape proposal* — it answers \"does this path's layout look like a\n    worktree path\", NOT \"is it a registered coord worktree\". Contract-label\n    consistency guards (status_service) use this; topology *routing* decisions\n    must use :func:`is_registered_coord_worktree` / :func:`classify_worktree_topology`,\n    which additionally consult the git registry so a husk cannot spoof the\n    authority.\n    \"\"\"\n    return _WORKTREES_SEGMENT in path.parts\n\n\n        kittify_path = candidate / KITTIFY_DIR\n        if kittify_path.is_symlink() and not kittify_path.exists():\n            # Broken symlink - skip this candidate\n            continue\n        if kittify_path.is_dir():\n            return candidate\n\n    return None\n\n\ndef lint_report_path(repo_root: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Return the canonical path of the repo-global charter-lint decay report.\n\n    The lint engine writes ``<repo_root>/.kittify/lint-report.json``; the\n    dashboard tile and the dossier stager read it back. This accessor is the\n    single source of truth for that location — no caller should re-compose the\n    ``.kittify`` / filename literals by hand (#2628 SSOT fold).\n    \"\"\"\n    return repo_root / KITTIFY_DIR / LINT_REPORT_FILENAME\n\n\ndef is_worktree_context(path: Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect if the given path is within a git worktree directory.\n\n    Checks two conditions:\n    1. '.worktrees' appears in the path hierarchy (spec-kitty managed worktrees)\n    2. The nearest .git entry is a file with a gitdir: pointer (generic git worktree)\n\n    Args:\n        path: Path to check (typically current working directory)\n\n    Returns:\n        True if path is within any git worktree, False otherwise\n\n    Examples:\n        >>> is_worktree_context(Path(\"/repo/.worktrees/feature-001\"))\n        True\n        >>> is_worktree_context(Path(\"/repo/kitty-specs\"))\n        False\n        >>> # Also detects external worktrees (e.g. under /tmp)\n        >>> is_worktree_context(Path(\"/tmp/my-worktree\"))  # if .git is a gitdir pointer\n        True\n    \"\"\"\n    # Fast path: spec-kitty managed worktrees\n    if WORKTREES_DIR in path.parts:\n        return True\n\n    # Generic detection: walk up to find .git file with gitdir pointer\n    # Only recognise true worktrees (.git/worktrees/<name> topology),\n    # NOT submodules (.git/modules/<mod>) or separate-git-dir clones.\n    resolved = path.resolve()\n    for candidate in [resolved, *resolved.parents]:\n        git_path = candidate / \".git\"\n        if git_path.is_file():\n            try:\n                content = git_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").strip()\n                if content.startswith(_GITDIR_PREFIX):\n                    gitdir = Path(content.split(\":\", 1)[1].strip())\n                    if _is_worktree_gitdir(gitdir):\n                        return True\n            except OSError:\n                pass\n            break\n        elif git_path.is_dir():\n            # Main repo .git directory — not a worktree\n            break\n\n    return False\n\n\ndef resolve_with_context(start: Path | None = None) -> tuple[Path | None, bool]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Resolve project root and detect worktree context in one call.\n\n    Args:\n        start: Starting directory for search (defaults to current working directory)\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (project_root, is_worktree)\n        - project_root: Path to repo root or None if not found\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

destination-ref-aware HEAD assertion.\n\nThis module provides utilities for committing only specific files without\ncapturing unrelated staged changes, while structurally enforcing that the\ncommit lands on the branch the caller declared.\n\nContract (post-#1348 / mission ``mission-coordination-branch-atomic-event-log``)\n-------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n``safe_commit()`` requires a **keyword-only** ``destination_ref`` (the short\nbranch name, never ``refs/heads/<name>``) and a ``worktree_root`` path. Before\nany staging or commit, the helper asserts that the worktree's ``HEAD`` matches\n``destination_ref``. There is **no silent fallback** path that infers the\ndestination from the current working directory or HEAD --- a missing argument\nfails ``mypy --strict``, and a mismatched HEAD raises ``SafeCommitHeadMismatch``.\n\nThis is the structural invariant that makes every caller correct-by-construction.\nPolicy can no longer drift from physical staging because policy and physical\ntarget are checked against each other at the chokepoint.\n\nEvery exception below carries a stable ``error_code`` (NFR-007) for scripted\ndetection, plus ``destination_ref`` and (where relevant) ``observed_head`` and\n``worktree_root`` so operators and CI tooling can act on structured data.\n\nStaging-area data-loss backstop\n-------------------------------\n\nIn addition to the destination-ref invariant, every ``safe_commit`` call still\nasserts that the staging area contains exactly the paths the caller requested\nbefore the commit is created. If any unexpected path is staged (for example, a\nphantom deletion produced by a sparse-checkout filter interacting with\n``git stash pop``), the commit is aborted with ``SafeCommitBackstopError``. The\nbackstop is unconditional and cannot be bypassed via any ``--force`` code path\n--- see Priivacy-ai/spec-kitty#588 for the cascade it defends against.\n\nProtected-branch authorization policy (FR-008)\n----------------------------------------------\n\nA commit may land on a protected branch ONLY when the caller asserts a\nprotected-flow :class:`~specify_cli.core.commit_guard.GuardCapability` at the\ncall site (``release_flow`` / ``upgrade_bookkeeping`` / ``merge_bookkeeping`` /\n``test_mode``). The decision is made solely by ``commit_guard.evaluate`` —\nauthorization is asserted-at-the-surface, never derived from commit-message\ntext, committed-file content, or ambient environment.\n\nWP03 DELETED the historical privilege channels that used to grant this implicitly:\n\n- the message-prefix allowlist (``release: ``/``chore: …`` etc.) — the upgrade,\n  release, and merge-bookkeeping flows now pass an explicit capability;\n- the two ``allow_*`` protected-branch bool parameters and the op-record JSONL\n  file-content exception — folded into ``GuardCapability.test_mode`` /\n  ``merge_bookkeeping``;\n- the ``SPEC_KITTY_TEST_MODE`` env privilege hatch.\n\nThe ONE retained operator escape hatch,\n``SPEC_KITTY_ALLOW_PROTECTED_BRANCH_COMMITS`` (for solo-fork operators who own\n``main``), is consumed by the protected-branch pre-checks AND by\n``safe_commit``'s ``ProtectionState`` input computation — the operator declares\nthe branch unprotected for this repo. ``commit_guard.evaluate`` itself never\nreads the environment.\n\nSpec-kitty-internal exceptions (planning-artifact prefixes such as\n``\"chore: planning artifacts for \"``) were removed as part of #1348 (FR-013):\nthey constituted a silent bypass. New protected-branch flows require a\ncapability, not a message convention.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom specify_cli.core.constants import KITTY_SPECS_DIR, WORKTREES_DIR\nimport contextlib\nimport logging\nimport subprocess\nimport uuid\nfrom collections.abc import Sequence\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom mission_runtime import CommitTarget\nfrom kernel.paths import to_posix\nfrom specify_cli.core.commit_guard import GuardCapability, GuardVerdict, ProtectionState\nfrom kernel.clock import now_utc_iso\nfrom specify_cli.core.commit_guard import evaluate as evaluate_commit_guard\nfrom specify_cli.git.protection_policy import ProtectionPolicy\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Structured error types (NFR-007: each carries a stable ``error_code``)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n# Adopt-on-next-touch: this family predates the shared\n# ``specify_cli.core.errors.StructuredError`` base (#1893). Reparent onto it\n# (overriding ``to_dict`` for the extra contextual fields) the next time this\n# class is materially edited.\nclass SafeCommitError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"Base class for structured safe_commit errors.\n\n    Subclasses set ``error_code`` to a stable identifier. Every instance\n    exposes JSON-serializable fields via :meth:`to_dict` for CI / scripted\n    detection (NFR-007).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    error_code: str = \"SAFE_COMMIT_GENERIC\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        message: str,\n        *,\n        destination_ref: str | None = None,\n        observed_head: str | None = None,\n        worktree_root: Path | None = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        super().__init__(message)\n        self.message = message\n        self.destination_ref = destination_ref\n        self.observed_head = observed_head\n        self.worktree_root = worktree_root\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serializable representation for tooling.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"error_code\": self.error_code,\n            \"message\": self.message,\n            \"destination_ref\": self.destination_ref,\n            \"observed_head\": self.observed_head,\n            \"worktree_root\": str(self.worktree_root) if self.worktree_root is not None else None,\n        }\n\n\nclass SafeCommitHeadMismatch(SafeCommitError):\n    \"\"\"Worktree HEAD does not match the declared ``destination_ref``.\"\"\"\n\n    error_code = \"SAFE_COMMIT_HEAD_MISMATCH\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        destination_ref: str,\n        observed_head: str,\n        worktree_root: Path,\n    ) -> None:\n        message = (\n            f\"safe_commit: worktree {worktree_root} HEAD is {observed_head!r}, \"\n            f\"expected {destination_ref!r}. \"\n            f\"Run `git -C {worktree_root} checkout {destination_ref}` first.\"\n        )\n        super().__init__(\n            message,\n            destination_ref=destination_ref,\n            observed_head=observed_head,\n            worktree_root=worktree_root,\n        )\n\n\nclass SafeCommitDestinationRefShape(SafeCommitError):\n    \"\"\"``destination_ref`` was passed in fully-qualified form (``refs/heads/...``).\n\n    The contract requires the **short** branch name. Callers must normalize at\n    the boundary so the helper can do a single shape-agnostic comparison. Per\n    C-016.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    error_code = \"SAFE_COMMIT_DESTINATION_REF_SHAPE\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *, destination_ref: str) -> None:\n        message = (\n            f\"safe_commit: destination_ref must be a short branch name, \"\n            f\"got fully-qualified ref {destination_ref!r}. \"\n            f\"Strip the 'refs/heads/' prefix at the call boundary.\"\n        )\n        super().__init__(message, destination_ref=destination_ref)\n\n\nclass SafeCommitDestinationNotFound(SafeCommitError):\n    \"\"\"``destination_ref`` does not exist as a branch in the repository.\"\"\"\n\n            capture_output=True,\n            text=True,\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n            errors=\"replace\",\n            check=False,\n        )\n        if add_result.returncode != 0:\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef _staged_patch_for_paths(repo_path: Path, normalized_files: list[str]) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return an exact binary patch for currently-staged requested paths.\"\"\"\n    if not normalized_files:\n        return \"\"\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"diff\", \"--cached\", \"--binary\", \"--no-ext-diff\", \"--no-renames\", \"--\", *normalized_files],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        return None\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef _unstage_requested_files(repo_path: Path, normalized_files: list[str]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Remove requested paths from the index before saving unrelated staging.\"\"\"\n    if not normalized_files:\n        return\n\n    staged_result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"diff\", \"--cached\", \"--name-only\", \"--\", *normalized_files],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    if staged_result.returncode != 0:\n        return\n    staged_requested = [line.strip() for line in staged_result.stdout.splitlines() if line.strip()]\n    if not staged_requested:\n        return\n\n    has_head = _run_git_text(repo_path, [\"rev-parse\", \"--verify\", \"HEAD\"]) is not None\n    if has_head:\n        subprocess.run(\n            [\"git\", \"restore\", \"--staged\", \"--\", *staged_requested],\n            cwd=repo_path,\n            capture_output=True,\n            text=True,\n            encoding=\"utf-8\",\n            errors=\"replace\",\n            check=False,\n        )\n        return\n\n    subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"rm\", \"--cached\", \"--ignore-unmatch\", \"-q\", \"--\", *staged_requested],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n\n\ndef _restore_staged_patch(\n    repo_path: Path,\n    normalized_files: list[str],\n    patch: str | None,\n    *,\n    destination_ref: str | None = None,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Restore the caller's pre-existing staged requested-file state.\"\"\"\n    if patch is None:\n        raise SafeCommitRecoveryFailed(\n            f\"safe_commit: failed to restore caller staging in {repo_path}; \"\n            \"requested-file staged patch was not captured before index mutation.\",\n            destination_ref=destination_ref,\n            worktree_root=repo_path,\n            unrecovered_paths=normalized_files,\n        )\n    _unstage_requested_files(repo_path, normalized_files)\n    if not patch:\n        return\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"apply\", \"--cached\", \"--whitespace=nowarn\", \"-\"],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        input=patch,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        detail = (result.stderr or result.stdout).strip()\n        suffix = f\": {detail}\" if detail else \".\"\n        raise SafeCommitRecoveryFailed(\n            f\"safe_commit: failed to restore caller staging in {repo_path}; \"\n            f\"git apply --cached rejected the requested-file patch{suffix}\",\n            destination_ref=destination_ref,\n            worktree_root=repo_path,\n            unrecovered_paths=normalized_files,\n        )\n\n\n_EMPTY_CHANGESET_MARKERS = (\n    \"nothing to commit\",\n    \"nothing added to commit\",\n    \"no changes added to commit\",\n)\n\n\ndef _commit_output_is_empty_changeset(output: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True iff git's own output says the commit was a genuine empty changeset.\n\n    Secondary signal only — see :func:`_staged_tree_is_empty` for the\n    authoritative check. Output-text matching alone is unsound: a failing\n    pre-commit hook can print one of these markers to its own stdout/stderr\n    while rejecting a real staged change, which would misclassify a genuine\n    failure as a benign no-op (audit finding, PR #3269). Kept as a fallback\n    for callers that only have the combined text and no repo to probe.\n    \"\"\"\n    low = output.lower()\n    return any(marker in low for marker in _EMPTY_CHANGESET_MARKERS)\n\n\ndef _staged_tree_is_empty(repo_path: Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True iff the index matches HEAD, i.e. there is genuinely nothing staged.\n\n    This is the AUTHORITY for the empty-vs-failure decision after a failed\n    ``git commit`` (audit BLOCK_MATERIAL, PR #3269): git's own combined\n    stdout+stderr text is not a reliable signal, because a pre-commit hook\n    that REJECTS a real staged change can still print a \"nothing to commit\"\n    -shaped message on its own account. A hook failure always leaves the\n    rejected files staged, so the index still differs from HEAD regardless of\n    what strings the hook printed -- while a true no-op leaves the index\n    identical to HEAD. Keying off staged state instead of output text makes\n    the distinction structural rather than textual.\n\n    Runs ``git diff --cached --quiet`` in ``repo_path``: exit code 0 means the\n    staged tree matches HEAD (nothing to commit); exit code 1 means staged\n    content differs from HEAD (a real, non-empty change is sitting in the\n    index). Any other exit code is treated as \"not empty\" (fail closed --\n    do not mask a genuine failure as a benign no-op just because the probe\n    itself misbehaved).\n    \"\"\"\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"diff\", \"--cached\", \"--quiet\"],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    return result.returncode == 0\n\n\ndef _run_commit_capture_sha(repo_path: Path, commit_message: str) -> tuple[str | None, str]:\n    \"\"\"Run ``git commit``.\n\n    Returns ``(new SHA, \"\")`` on success, or ``(None, combined_output)`` on\n    failure — where ``combined_output`` carries git's stdout+stderr so the\n    caller can tell an empty changeset apart from a genuine commit failure\n    (e.g. a failing pre-commit hook) instead of collapsing both to ``None``.\n    \"\"\"\n    commit_result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"-c\", \"commit.gpgsign=false\", \"commit\", \"-m\", commit_message],\n        cwd=repo_path,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        encoding=\"utf-8\",\n        errors=\"replace\",\n        check=False,\n    )\n    if commit_result.returncode != 0:\n        combined = f\"{commit_result.stdout}\\n{commit_result.stderr}\".strip()\n        return None, combined\n    sha = _run_git_text(repo_path, [\"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"])\n    return sha, \"\"\n\n\ndef _derive_mission_id(paths: list[str]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Extract the mission slug from the first path under ``kitty-specs/``.\n\n    For example, ``kitty-specs/my-mission-01KT119Y/file.jsonl`` → ``my-mission-01KT119Y``.\n    Returns ``\"\"`` if extraction fails.\n    \"\"\"\n    for p in paths:\n        parts = Path(p).parts\n        for i, part in enumerate(parts):\n            if part == KITTY_SPECS_DIR and i + 1 < len(parts):\n                return parts[i + 1]\n    return \"\"\n\n\ndef _get_current_build_id(repo_root: Path) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the session-level build_id if available; fall back to ``generate_build_id()``.\n\n    Reads the project identity from ``.kittify/config.yaml`` (stored by\n    ``spec-kitty init``). generate_build_id, load_identity  # noqa: PLC0415\n\n        config_path = repo_root / \".kittify\" / \"config.yaml\"\n        identity = load_identity(config_path)\n        if identity.build_id:\n During /spec-kitty.implement this WP may branch from a dependency-specific base, but completed changes must merge back into fix/worktree-owned-root-3328-v2 unless the human explicitly redirects the landing branch.\nsubtasks:\n- T006\n- T007\n- T008\n- T009\nhistory:\n- at: '2026-08-11T13:37:00Z'\n  actor: system\n  action: Prompt generated via /spec-kitty.tasks-packages\nagent_profile: ''\nauthoritative_surface: src/specify_cli/core/mission_creation.py\ncreate_intent: []\nexecution_mode: code_change\nmodel: ''\nowned_files:\n- src/specify_cli/cli/commands/agent/mission_create.py\n- src/specify_cli/core/mission_creation.py\n- tests/agent/test_agent_feature.py\nrole: implementer\ntags: []\ntracker_refs: []\n---\n\n# Work Package Prompt: WP02 - mission create explicit-ownership integration\n\n## Objective\n\nAdd a new, explicit, validated CLI affordance to `spec-kitty agent mission create` that lets a caller declare the invoking worktree as the mission's owned root, using WP01's `resolve_ownership_claim()` — WITHOUT touching `allow_worktree_context` (FR-010/NFR-003) and WITHOUT changing behavior for any caller that omits the new flag (FR-004/C-001/C-002).\n\n## Context\n\nToday, `create_mission_core()` (`src/specify_cli/core/mission_creation.py:206-321`) refuses unconditionally when `Path.cwd()` is inside a worktree (line 309-314), regardless of what `repo_root` was passed. The refusal is disconnected from `repo_root` resolution entirely. This WP adds a new keyword parameter (see `contracts/checkout-ownership-cli-contract.md` for the exact `--owned-checkout` flag contract) that, when supplied, is validated via WP01's primitive BEFORE the existing guard fires — and only bypasses the guard when validation returns `OWNED`.\n\nRead `plan.md` IC-02 and `contracts/checkout-ownership-cli-contract.md` in full before starting.\n\n**Critical existing behavior to preserve** (regression net = `tests/agent/test_agent_feature.py::test_blocks_create_feature_from_worktree_with_main_repo_hint` and `::test_blocks_create_feature_from_worktree_with_worktrees_fallback_hint`, plus `tests/specify_cli/core/test_feature_creation.py::test_worktree_context_raises`): every one of these must still pass UNCHANGED — they exercise the no-opt-in path.\n\n## Branch Strategy\n\n- **Strategy**: {{branch_strategy}}\n- **Planning base branch**: {{planning_base_branch}}\n- **Merge target branch**: {{merge_target_branch}}\n\n## Subtasks & Detailed Guidance\n\n### Subtask T006 - Add `--owned-checkout` to the CLI surface\n\n- **Purpose**: Expose the new affordance on `spec-kitty agent mission create`.\n- **Steps**:\n  1. In `src/specify_cli/cli/commands/agent/mission_create.py`, add a new `typer.Option` parameter `owned_checkout: Path | None = typer.Option(None, \"--owned-checkout\", help=\"...\")` to `create_mission` (the function currently spanning lines ~404-533).\n  2. Thread it into the call to `create_mission_core()` (the `_run_create_core_phase` helper, lines 250-309) as a new keyword — do NOT reuse or alias `allow_worktree_context`.\n  3. Resolve `owned_checkout` with `.resolve()` before passing it down (consistent with how `repo_root` is resolved elsewhere in this file).\n- **Files**: `src/specify_cli/cli/commands/agent/mission_create.py` (~20 line diff)\n- **Parallel?**: Sequenced before T007 (same file, different function).\n\n### Subtask T007 - Wire `create_mission_core()` to consult WP01's primitive\n\n- **Purpose**: Replace the unconditional refusal with a validated branch.\n- **Steps**:\n  1. In `create_mission_core()` (`src/specify_cli/core/mission_creation.py:206-321`), add a new keyword parameter `owned_checkout: Path | None = None` (distinct from the existing `allow_worktree_context: bool = False` — do NOT remove or rename that parameter; it stays for its existing test-only purpose).\n  2. Before the existing guard (lines 309-314), if `owned_checkout is not None`: call `resolve_ownership_claim(owned_checkout, resolved_primary=<the already-being-resolved repo_root>)`. On `OWNED`, skip the `is_worktree_context` refusal entirely for this invocation and proceed using `owned_checkout` as the effective worktree root for the write path (T008). On any other result, raise the corresponding structured error (see FR-011 / contracts) BEFORE reaching the existing generic `MissionCreationError`.\n  3. When `owned_checkout is None`, behavior must be BYTE-IDENTICAL to today — the existing guard at lines 309-314 fires exactly as before.\n- **Files**: `src/specify_cli/core/mission_creation.py` (~40 line diff)\n- **Validation**: Re-run `tests/specify_cli/core/test_feature_creation.py::test_worktree_context_raises` — must still pass unmodified.\n\n### Subtask T008 - Thread `owned_checkout` into `safe_commit` as `worktree_root`\n\n- **Purpose**: Satisfy FR-008/FR-009 — mission-create writes must land in the owned checkout, not always `repo_root==repo_root`.\n- **Steps**:\n  1. In `_commit_feature_file()` (`mission_creation.py:155-198`), when an `OwnershipClaim` with `validation_result == OWNED` is present for this invocation, call `safe_commit(repo_root=<canonical resolved_primary>, worktree_root=<owned_checkout>, ...)` — mirroring `coordination/transaction.py`'s `BookkeepingTransaction` pattern (research D-5) rather than the current always-equal call.\n  2. When no `OwnershipClaim` is present (default path), keep the existing `safe_commit(repo_root=repo_root, worktree_root=repo_root, ...)` call exactly as today.\n  3. Confirm mission-directory scaffolding (spec.md/meta.json/tasks/README.md writes) is written under `owned_checkout`, not `resolved_primary`, when ownership is `OWNED` (FR-008: mission/ref write isolation).\n- **Files**: `src/specify_cli/core/mission_creation.py` (~30 line diff, same file as T007 — sequence after)\n- **Validation**: Manual check per `quickstart.md` step 3 — inspect that the created mission's files exist under the owned checkout path, and `git status --short` in the primary checkout remains clean.\n\n### Subtask T009 - Regression tests for the unchanged default path + new opted-in path\n\n- **Purpose**: Prove FR-004/C-001/C-002 hold and FR-001 works.\n- **Steps**:\n  1. In `tests/agent/test_agent_feature.py`, add a new test asserting `--owned-checkout` from a real linked worktree (via `tmp_path` + `git worktree add`) succeeds and the mission lands under that worktree.\n  2. Confirm the three EXISTING worktree-refusal tests in this file still pass with ZERO modification to their assertions (only add new tests; do not touch the existing ones beyond what's needed to keep them passing after T006/T007's signature changes).\n  3. Add a test for the `NESTED`/`FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED` refusal surfaced through the CLI (`--json` output `error_code`), per `contracts/checkout-ownership-cli-contract.md`.\n- **Files**: `tests/agent/test_agent_feature.py` (~80 line diff)\n\n## Test Strategy\n\n- `.venv/bin/pytest tests/agent/test_agent_feature.py tests/specify_cli/core/test_feature_creation.py tests/core/test_mission_create_activation_gate.py -q`\n- Manually run `quickstart.md` steps 2-3 and 7 (no-opt-in + nested/foreign cases) against a local build.\n\n## Risks & Mitigations\n\n- **Risk**: `_print_worktree_navigation_hint`'s substring match on `\"worktree\"` (mission_create.py:228-247) could mis-fire for the NEW error classes if their messages also contain the word \"worktree\". **Mitigation**: gate that hint's substring match to ONLY the unchanged `MissionCreationError` type, not the new structured exceptions from WP01.\n- **Risk**: Changing `create_mission_core()`'s signature could break `tests/_factories/__init__.py::make_mission()`'s keyword-forwarding. **Mitigation**: add `owned_checkout` as a new, optional, keyword-only parameter at the end of the signature — never reorder existing parameters.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n- [ ] `--owned-checkout` succeeds for a real linked worktree at a generic (non-`.worktrees`) path.\n- [ ] All pre-existing worktree-refusal tests pass unmodified.\n- [ ] `NESTED`/`FOREIGN_OR_MISMATCHED`/`BROKEN_POINTER` surface as distinguishable `--json` `error_code` values.\n- [ ] No production call site newly introduces `allow_worktree_context=True` (verify with `tests/architectural/test_no_production_worktree_guard_bypass.py`).\n\n## Reviewer Guidance\n\n- Confirm `allow_worktree_context` and `owned_checkout` are fully independent parameters — no aliasing, no shared code path that could be confused for the other.\n- Confirm the mission's files genuinely land under the owned checkout (not This WP does not change `require_main_repo`'s existing detection scope — it adds a parallel, explicit path that is consulted first.\n\nRead `plan.md` IC-03 and `contracts/checkout-ownership-cli-contract.md` (\"CLI Surface: spec-kitty next\" section) in full before starting. Add a test: `next` from a generic (non-`.worktrees`) linked worktree WITHOUT `--owned-checkout` behaves identically before and after this WP's changes (byte-comparable decision output, aside from timestamps/run IDs) — this is the explicit regression proof for spec.md User Story 2 Acceptance Scenario #4.\n- **Files**: `tests/agent/test_context_validation_unit.py` (~60 line diff)\n\n## Test Strategy\n\n- `.venv/bin/pytest tests/agent/test_context_validation_unit.py tests/runtime/ -q`\n- Manually run `quickstart.md` step 5 against a local build.\n\n## Risks & Mitigations\n\n- **Risk**: The `@require_main_repo` decorator wraps `next_step` at the function-definition level (line 60) — a naive \"check the flag inside the function body\" approach runs AFTER the decorator's refusal already fired for `.worktrees`-literal paths. **Mitigation**: either (a) have `require_main_repo` itself accept/check a resolved `OwnershipClaim` passed via a context object before its own comparison, or (b) restructure so ownership resolution happens before the decorator is invoked (e.g., a thin undecorated dispatcher that checks ownership first, then calls the decorated implementation only on the non-opted-in path). Document which you chose and why in this WP's Activity Log — this is the one open architectural decision this plan deliberately left to implementation (plan.md IC-03 Risks).\n- **Risk**: Merge/implement also use `@require_main_repo` (research D-1) — this WP does NOT extend `--owned-checkout` to those commands (out of scope; spec.md is scoped to `mission create`/`next` only). Confirm no accidental scope creep.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n- [ ] `next --owned-checkout <path>` succeeds for a real linked worktree and roots runtime state there.\n- [ ] `next` without `--owned-checkout` is behaviorally unchanged for both `.worktrees`-literal and generic linked-worktree callers.\n- [ ] The status-lock relocation exclusion is respected (verified by a test asserting the lock path is unchanged).\n- [ ] Architectural decision on the decorator-vs-ownership-check ordering is documented in the Activity Log below.\n\n## Reviewer Guidance\n\n- Verify the regression proof for generic-linked-worktree-without-opt-in explicitly (this is the easiest requirement to accidentally regress, since it requires proving a NEGATIVE — that nothing changed).\n- Verify `merge`/`implement` were not touched (scope discipline).\n\n**Implementation command**: `spec-kitty agent action implement WP03 --agent <name>`\n\n## Activity Log\n\n- 2026-08-11T13:37:00Z - system - Prompt created.\n---\nwork_package_id: WP04\ntitle: Structured refusal errors, --json contract, and architectural fence reconciliation\ndependencies:\n- WP02\n- WP03\nrequirement_refs:\n- FR-011\n- NFR-003\nplanning_base_branch: fix/worktree-owned-root-3328-v2\nmerge_target_branch: fix/worktree-owned-root-3328-v2\nbranch_strategy: Planning artifacts for this mission were generated on fix/worktree-owned-root-3328-v2. This function can only run from main repo\n        pass\n\n    @require_worktree\n    def some_workspace_command():\n        # This function can only run from inside a worktree\n        pass\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport functools\nimport os\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom enum import StrEnum\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import TypeVar\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\n\nimport typer\nfrom rich.console import Console\n\nconsole = Console()\n\n\nclass ExecutionContext(StrEnum):\n    \"\"\"Execution context for a command.\"\"\"\n\n    MAIN_REPO = \"main\"  # Command runs in main repository\n    WORKTREE = \"worktree\"  # Command runs inside a worktree\n    EITHER = \"either\"  # Command can run in either location\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass CurrentContext:\n    \"\"\"Current execution context information.\"\"\"\n\n    location: ExecutionContext\n    cwd: Path\n    repo_root: Path | None\n    worktree_name: str | None  # e.g., \"010-feature-lane-a\" if in worktree\n    worktree_path: Path | None  # Absolute path to worktree directory\n\n\ndef detect_execution_context(cwd: Path | None = None) -> CurrentContext:\n    \"\"\"Detect current execution context.\n\n    Args:\n        cwd: Current working directory (defaults to Path.cwd())\n\n    Returns:\n        CurrentContext with location, paths, and worktree info\n\n    Example:\n        >>> ctx = detect_execution_context()\n        >>> if ctx.location == ExecutionContext.WORKTREE:\n        ...     print(f\"In worktree: {ctx.worktree_name}\")\n    \"\"\"\n    cwd = Path.cwd().resolve() if cwd is None else cwd.resolve()\n\n    # Check if .worktrees is in path\n    if \".worktrees\" in cwd.parts:\n        # Extract worktree information\n        for i, part in enumerate(cwd.parts):\n            if part == \".worktrees\" and i + 1 < len(cwd.parts):\n                candidate_root = Path(*cwd.parts[:i])\n                if (candidate_root / \".kittify\").exists() or (candidate_root / \".git\").exists():\n                    worktree_name = cwd.parts[i + 1]\n                    worktree_path = Path(*cwd.parts[: i + 2])\n                    repo_root = candidate_root\n\n                    return CurrentContext(\n                        location=ExecutionContext.WORKTREE,\n                        cwd=cwd,\n                        repo_root=repo_root,\n                        worktree_name=worktree_name,\n                        worktree_path=worktree_path,\n                    )\n\n    # Not in worktree - assume main repo\n    # Try to find repo root (directory containing .kittify or .git)\n    repo_root = None\n    search_path = cwd\n    for _ in range(10):  # Limit depth\n        if (search_path / \".kittify\").exists() or (search_path / \".git\").exists():\n            repo_root = search_path\n            break\n        if search_path.parent == search_path:\n            break  # Reached filesystem root\n        search_path = search_path.parent\n\n    return CurrentContext(\n        location=ExecutionContext.MAIN_REPO,\n        cwd=cwd,\n        repo_root=repo_root,\n        worktree_name=None,\n        worktree_path=None,\n    )\n\n\ndef get_current_context() -> CurrentContext:\n    \"\"\"Get current execution context.\n\n    Convenience function that detects context from current working directory.\n\n    Returns:\n        CurrentContext with location and path information\n    \"\"\"\n    return detect_execution_context()\n\n\ndef format_location_error(\n    required: ExecutionContext,\n    actual: ExecutionContext,\n    command_name: str,\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

error distinguishing \"nested/foreign topology\" from the plain \"invoked from a worktree\" case — it must NOT fall through to ambient-primary resolution.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-60-3. **Given** a foreign worktree — a linked worktree of a *different* git repository entirely (unrelated common-dir) — pointed at as the target via the new flag, **When** the command runs, **Then** it refuses; the mismatched common-dir is named in the error.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-61-4. **Given** a generic linked worktree path (not under `.worktrees/`) invoking `next` **without** the new ownership affordance, **When** `next` runs, **Then** behavior is unchanged from pre-mission baseline (ambient resolution to primary, exactly as today) — this mission does not retrofit `next`'s existing unscoped behavior into a refusal for callers who never opted in; it only adds the explicit, validated opt-in Entities\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-129-\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-130-- **Owned checkout**: The specific git worktree (primary or linked) that a mission-mutating command has been explicitly and validly told to treat as its write root for a given invocation. Distinguished from \"canonical common repository topology\" (the shared object store/common-dir all worktrees of one repo share) and from \"ambiently resolved primary\" (today's default collapse target).\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md:131:- **Checkout ownership validation result**: The outcome of comparing the invoking worktree's git identity (its `.git` gitdir pointer, its own `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` / `--git-common-dir`) against the resolved primary's common-dir — one of: owned (accepted), unowned-no-opt-in (existing refusal, unchanged), nested (new refusal), foreign/mismatched (new refusal), broken-pointer (new refusal).\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-132-- **Per-checkout runtime state**: Runtime bookkeeping (`feature-runs.json`-equivalent, merge-lock directories) rooted under the owned checkout's own `.kittify/runtime/` rather than the ambiently-resolved primary's — distinct from the intentionally-shared cross-worktree status lock (`status/locking.py`, which stays rooted at the shared common-dir by design and is out of scope for relocation).\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-133-\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-134-## Success Criteria *(mandatory)*\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-135-\n--\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-137-\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-138-- **SC-001**: An agent in any real linked worktree (any path, not only `.worktrees/<name>`) can explicitly create and advance a mission rooted in that worktree, with zero files or state appearing in the primary checkout or any sibling worktree, verified by `git status --short` clean in all trees.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-139-- **SC-002**: Two agents running the real installed CLI concurrently in two separate linked worktrees, with forced temporal overlap, produce two missions with zero shared/collided identifiers (mission ID, slug, ref names, runtime-state file contents) across 20 consecutive deterministic runs.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-140-- **SC-003**: Every caller that does not explicitly request worktree ownership is refused or behaves exactly as it does today — zero observable regression in the existing `mission create` / `next` / `merge` / `implement` worktree-invocation behavior for non-opted-in callers.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md:141:- **SC-004**: 100% of the four defined negative/adversarial ownership-validation scenarios (nested, foreign, broken-pointer, opt-in-without-validation-passing) produce a distinguishable refusal, verified by dedicated tests.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/spec.md-142-- **SC-005**: The end-to-end concurrency proof runs against an immutable installed artifact whose provenance (source commit SHA, wheel SHA-256, build options) is recorded and reproducible — zero acceptance of editable-install or mocked-CLI evidence.\n--\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/quickstart.md-66-7. **Verify negative/refusal |\n--\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-31-\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-32-- `src/specify_cli/git/commit_helpers.py:609-634`. Compares `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` and `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` between the candidate worktree and the candidate repo root. Generic: works for any linked worktree.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-33-- Currently invoked only as step 3 of `safe_commit()`'s validation chain (`commit_helpers.py:904+`), i.e., after mission-create has already decided whether to proceed. There is no pre-flight call to this (or an equivalent) at the point `mission create`/`next` decide whether to accept worktree ownership.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-34-- **Decision**: FR-003/FR-005/FR-006 reuse this exact predicate (or a thin wrapper around it) as the pre-flight ownership-validation primitive, rather than writing a fifth independent common-dir comparison. This directly satisfies C-006 (generic linked-worktree recognition) since `_is_worktree_of` already has no `.worktrees`-literal dependency.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md:35:- **Nested-worktree detection**: `_is_worktree_of` alone does not distinguish \"sibling worktree of the same repo\" from \"worktree nested inside another worktree's own checkout directory\" — both share the same common-dir. FR-005 (refuse nested topology) requires an additional check: is the invoking worktree's own root path a descendant of ANY other worktree's root (per `git worktree list --porcelain`)? This is where `coordination/surface_resolver.read_worktree_registry()` (`src/specify_cli/coordination/surface_resolver.py:231-262`) is directly reusable — it already shells `git worktree list --porcelain` and fails closed (`WorktreeRegistryUnavailable`) on git failure, satisfying NFR-004. Its own `_enclosing_worktree_root()` helper (lines 286-297) is `.worktrees`-literal and NOT reusable as-is for the nested check (C-006 gap), but the registry data it parses (`read_worktree_registry`) is topology-generic and IS reusable — the nested check should be rewritten against the raw registry entries' paths (prefix/ancestor comparison), not against `_enclosing_worktree_root`.\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-36-\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-37-## D-5: `safe_commit`'s `repo_root`/`worktree_root` split already exists and works — mission-create just never exercises it\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-38-\nkitty-specs/worktree-owned-root-3328-01KZRG01/research.md-39-- `safe_commit(*, repo_root: Path, worktree_root: Path, ...)` (`src/specify_cli/git/commit_helpers.py:904-913`). Docstring (958-960): `worktree_root` \"May equal `repo_root` when the primary checkout is the worktree.\"\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# The landscape places ``mission_runtime`` BELOW ``specify_cli``\n# (kernel <- ... <- mission_runtime <- specify_cli), so the clean invariant is\n# \"mission_runtime must NOT import specify_cli\". Today ``resolution.py`` (plus a\n# single edge in ``artifacts.py``) carry real *lazy, in-function* upward imports\n# into the ``specify_cli.*`` subpackages named below.\n#\n# PRE-DECIDED (research D6, renata-reviewed): these edges are a DOCUMENTED\n# allowed-exception set with recorded rationale — they are NOT violations. The\n# clean rule ``mission_runtime should_not access specify_cli`` would red on\n# existing, working code; converting the edges to hard errors is a carved-out\n# FUTURE mission (invert the dependency behind a port — infra/logic separation\n# epic #2173). This ledger is therefore bound as an outbound guard, not a purge.\n#\n# The ledger is SHRINK-ONLY by construction:\n#   * ADDING a new ``specify_cli.<sub>`` edge outside this set MUST red the rule\n#     (loud additions — proven by ``test_rule_rejects_out_of_ledger_import``),\n#   * REMOVING an edge (future port work) must delete its entry here; a stale\n#     entry with no matching live import reds ``test_ledger_has_no_stale_entries``.\n#\n# Each entry is a first-level ``specify_cli.<subpackage>`` name, derived from a\n# live AST scan of ``src/mission_runtime/`` (do NOT hand-copy from the plan).\n_MISSION_RUNTIME_ROOT = _SRC / \"mission_runtime\"\n\n_MISSION_RUNTIME_ALLOWED_SPECIFY_CLI: frozenset[str] = frozenset(\n    {\n        \"coordination\",      # resolution.py: CoordinationWorkspace, surface_resolver\n        \"core\",              # artifacts.py + resolution.py: constants, paths, dependency_graph\n        # coord-trust-2841: the \"lanes\" allow-row (resolution.py's coord-state\n        # branch resolving the mid8 disambiguator via\n        # ``lanes.branch_naming.resolve_mid8``, GEC-3 / contract C3) is CLOSED.\n        # ``resolve_mid8`` (and its heuristic sibling ``mid8_from_slug``) was\n        # pure and is now relocated to ``mission_runtime.identity``;\n        # ``specify_cli.lanes.branch_naming`` re-exports both verbatim so\n        # existing importers are unaffected. resolution.py imports the\n        # resolver directly from its new in-layer home — no specify_cli.lanes\n        # crossing remains.\n        \"migration\",         # resolution.py: backfill_topology.read_topology\n        \"mission\",           # resolution.py: get_mission_type\n        \"mission_metadata\",  # resolution.py: load_meta\n        \"missions\",          # resolution.py: _read_path_resolver\n        # coord-primary-partition-lock WP01 (H-1, binding): the placement seam's\n        # RETROSPECTIVE read leg MUST delegate to the SINGLE home authority\n        # ``retrospective.writer.resolve_retrospective_home`` (#2119) — computing\n        # a second RETROSPECTIVE home in mission_runtime would duplicate that\n\ndef _is_specify_cli_module(module: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True when ``module`` is ``specify_cli`` itself or one of its subpackages.\"\"\"\n    return module == \"specify_cli\" or module.startswith(\"specify_cli.\")\n\n\ndef _specify_cli_subpackage(module: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"First-level subpackage of a ``specify_cli`` import.\n\n    ``specify_cli.core.paths`` -> ``\"core\"``; bare ``specify_cli`` -> ``\"\"``.\n    \"\"\"\n    parts = module.split(\".\")\n    return parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else \"\"\n\n\ndef _collect_specify_cli_imports(root: Path) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(relative_path, imported_module)`` for every specify_cli import.\n\n    Walks the full AST so *lazy, in-function* imports are included — the\n    ``mission_runtime`` upward edges live inside functions, so a module-level\n    scan would miss them and the rule would pass vacuously.\n    \"\"\"\n    found: list[tuple[str, str]] = []\n    for path in sorted(root.rglob(\"*.py\")):\n        if \"__pycache__\" in path.parts:\n            continue\n        tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n        rel = str(path.relative_to(_SRC))\n        for node in ast.walk(tree):\n            if isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):\n                if node.module and _is_specify_cli_module(node.module):\n                    found.append((rel, node.module))\n            elif isinstance(node, ast.Import):\n                for alias in node.names:\n                    if _is_specify_cli_module(alias.name):\n                        found.append((rel, alias.name))\n    return found\n\n\ndef _out_of_ledger_specify_cli_imports(\n    imports: Iterable[tuple[str, str]],\n    # 0. Compat shim: accept either ``target`` (preferred) or the legacy\n    #    ``destination_ref`` string. The CommitTarget's ``ref`` is the single\n    #    destination authority; ``destination_ref`` mirrors it below so callers\n    #    not yet migrated to CommitTarget keep working. (Retiring this shim\n    #    requires converting the remaining string callers — out of WP03 scope.)\n    if target is not None and destination_ref is not None and target.ref != destination_ref:\n        raise SafeCommitDestinationRefShape(destination_ref=destination_ref)\n    if target is None:\n        if destination_ref is None:\n            raise SafeCommitEmptyChangeset(destination_ref=\"<none>\")\n        target = CommitTarget(ref=destination_ref)\n    destination_ref = target.ref\n\n    # 1. Shape: short branch name only.\n    if destination_ref.startswith(\"refs/heads/\"):\n        raise SafeCommitDestinationRefShape(destination_ref=destination_ref)\n\n    # 2. Non-empty paths.\n    if not paths:\n        raise SafeCommitEmptyChangeset(destination_ref=destination_ref)\n\n    # 3. worktree_root is a worktree of repo_root.\n    if not _is_worktree_of(repo_root, worktree_root):\n        raise SafeCommitNotAWorktree(\n            destination_ref=destination_ref,\n            worktree_root=worktree_root,\n        )\n\n    # 4. HEAD assertion.\n    observed_head = _read_worktree_head(worktree_root)\n    if observed_head is None or observed_head != destination_ref:\n        raise SafeCommitHeadMismatch(\n            destination_ref=destination_ref,\n            observed_head=observed_head if observed_head is not None else \"<detached>\",\n            worktree_root=worktree_root,\n        )\n\n    # 5. destination_ref exists.\n    if not _destination_ref_exists(worktree_root, destination_ref):\n        raise SafeCommitDestinationNotFound(\n            destination_ref=destination_ref,\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
